# toks end-to-end speed table

Generated by `tools/bench/e2e_table.py` from the raw logs in `docs/bench/raw/` (`tools/bench/e2e.sh` on each host;
never edit the numbers by hand). One thread, single process, MB/s = decimal megabytes of input per second.
host names are chipset keys (see docs/machines.md); home directories were replaced by $HOME in the receipts.

- **cells**: tokenizer x corpus x chunk. Corpora (`tools/bench/corpus.sha256`): **en** two Gutenberg novels (2.05 MB),
  **code** cpython + toks C sources (0.61 MB), **ml** wikipedia ar/de/el/fr/he/hi/ka/ru/ta/th/uk/vi + Gutenberg de/es/fr
  (6.6 MB), **cjk** wikipedia zh/ja/ko + a Gutenberg Chinese novel (3.1 MB). Chunk 4096 = each call gets the text
  up to the first newline at or past the next 4 KiB boundary; whole = the corpus in one call.
- **toks states** (`tools/bench/e2e.c`; cpu-cache residency is declared with each): **cold** = the
  scratch (dynamic piece cache) initialized before EVERY call, outside the timer, each call timed alone; logs from
  commit b3c92da on run the cold reps first, back to back (each cold pass starts with this text's lines in the cpu caches).
  Cold reps are cpu-cache-hot (back to back on one text); pass after other text is the serving state (a
  measure on gb10c cpu 8, master d11f9d1: coldo, each cold rep after an untimed pass over the other corpora, is 11-40%
  slower on code 4096 with identical K5 counters: llama3 425.0 -> 292.4, o200k 460.1 -> 277.3, qwen38 338.5 -> 259.8,
  gpt2 310.0 -> 277.1 MB/s; the loss scales with the static words table, 2 MiB gpt2 .. 8 MiB o200k, against the
  X925's 2 MiB L2). **coldo** (logs that have it): those cold reps, both tools. **warmo** (logs that have it): init,
  an untimed pass over the text, one over the other corpora, the timed pass over the text, no init between (the
  serving replay; not a same-language warm state, which is memo off on a disjoint corpus).
  The 5f71528 logs had no lang state, so their cold also followed a pass over the same text (the previous rep's
  warm); runs that did have one (defaults.sh's E2E_WARM_ON) ran cold right after the lang pass's sweep, which cost
  cold 6-15% (gb10c llama3 en 4096: 241.8 vs 256.9 MB/s at flags 0, 213.9 vs 250.6 at CACHE_MIB(16)):
  those cold numbers are not comparable with these. **pass** = one scratch over the
  corpus after an untimed pass over OTHER text (every bench corpus file outside the measured corpus, cut at 4096
  bytes whatever the cell's chunk), the scratch
  re-initialized before the timed pass: an empty piece cache filling as the pass goes, the cpu caches holding the
  other text's lines; **pass-same** (the pass of the logs at 5f71528 and earlier) = the
  same pass right after the cold pass over the same text, its lines still in the caches (llama3 4096 gb10a: code
  1.37 ms pass-same vs 1.82 ms pass). **warm** = the pass right after the pass: an exact replay from each tool's
  default cache budget as measured (toks: a 2 MiB piece cache, and from 0.3.0 the 4 MiB segment memo; gigatoken: 512
  MiB per state, its unit memo for
  sentencepiece), so warm vs warm is an unmatched comparison; **lang-x** (logs that have it) = the timed pass
  after an untimed pass over the other corpora on the same scratch (tools/bench/tokv1.sh's rule; cross-language:
  not SPEC §12.3's same-language warm-lang bar). toks runs hf's default call (added tokens
  recognized, post-processor applied). Best of reps per state.
- **comparators** on exactly the same chunks: **hf** tokenizers 0.23.2 `Tokenizer.encode` (Kimi K3: its own
  reference, transformers' TikTokenTokenizer, serving path); **tiktoken** 0.14.0 `encode_ordinary` over an Encoding
  built from the file's vocab and Split pattern (byte-level files only); **gigatoken** (the bar to beat; pinned
  source, its Rust API, `tools/bench/gigatoken`) in the same states: cold = a fresh state (fork / new
  EncodeState) before every call, cold reps first; pass = a state encodes the other text untimed, then a fresh
  state times the pass; warm = replay; lang-x = a fresh state warmed on the other text, then timed. hf and
  tiktoken run as they ship (their own caches warm, Python call overhead included). All times are best of reps.
  Kimi K3's gigatoken column: gigatoken-bench loads the model with `load_tiktoken(.., PretokenizerType::Kimi, [])`,
  without the wrapper's cuts (400,000-char chunks, 25,000-char same-class runs) and without specials, so its ids
  can differ from the reference on texts over 400,000 chars or with a 25,000-char run: the kimik3 en / code / ml
  whole cells are "n/a (ids differ)" for that reason (tiktoken's `encode_ordinary` likewise), not a toks defect;
  cjk whole happens to agree; a 4 KiB chunk holds no cut, so the 4096 cells are exact.
- **exactness**, outside every timer: toks ids == the reference's ids (sha-256 of the whole id stream), else the
  cell is VOID and in no ratio; tiktoken and gigatoken count only where their ids equal hf's without the
  post-processor (else n/a: "ids differ", or plain n/a where the file is not one byte-level Split pattern, e.g.
  gemma 4's sentencepiece-style bpe). Ratios use the fastest comparator time of the same host.
- **receipts** (end of this file): host, cpu, os, pin + governor, commit, compiler, kernels, binary / tokenizer /
  corpus sha-256, load (1 min) before -> after every cell and, on linux, the busy share of the pinned cpu and its
  smt sibling over the cell. Not a certified cell: no abba pairs, no bootstrap intervals.

Reproduce: `tools/release/rc.sh` (all hosts) or, on one host, `sh tools/bench/gigatoken.sh` then
`PINCPU=12 GIT_SHA=<sha> tools/bench/e2e.sh "taskset -c 12" > log`, then this script.

## Headline: 4 KiB chunks

MB/s. toks / x = toks MB/s over the comparator's (cold vs hf and tiktoken; cold vs cold and warm vs warm for gigatoken).

## Headline: whole corpus in one call

MB/s. toks / x = toks MB/s over the comparator's (cold vs hf and tiktoken; cold vs cold and warm vs warm for gigatoken).

## Incumbent: tok v1

The incumbent bar: every cell tok v1 supports must be faster in toks. tok v1 = the incumbent tokenizer at the commit
in the receipts (x86-64 asm with avx-512; arm64 asm with sve2 + neon), its image packed by its own tool from the
same tokenizer.json (qwen38: image version 3; glm53, nemotron3-omni: version 4). Its source is not public:
`tools/bench/tokv1.sh` (E_DIR = a checkout of it: an optional bench input, never a build or test dependency) runs
`tools/bench/tokv1.c`: one binary, one pinned core, both libraries on the same chunks, the sides alternating from
rep to rep (paired), every call's ids compared, fresh and warm, outside the timers (a difference voids the cell).
MB/s = the median of reps; **x** = the median of the per-rep ratios tok v1 time / toks time with its 95% bootstrap
interval: > 1 = toks faster. **toks** = 2 MiB piece caches, memo off (flags 0 before 0.3.0,
`TOKS_SCRATCH_MEMO_MIB(0)` since), **toks32** = `TOKS_SCRATCH_CACHE_MIB(32)`, memo off (a long-lived worker
scratch), **toksm** = `TOKS_SCRATCH_MEMO_MIB(4)` (toks's 4 MiB segment memo: the incumbent's flags, and toks'
default since 0.3.0). States: **cold** and **pass** as above; **lang** =
initialized, warmed on the other corpora (never a measured file), then timed: the incumbent's "new prompt in a warm worker",
tok v1's own headline state; **warm** = the same text again, where tok v1's and toksm's 4 MiB segment memos
answer every chunk they recorded (**memo** = the share of tok v1's pieces it skipped): a replay, not a
piece-cache race. **chat** = one conversation of 24 turns
(1-4 KiB of the en text each, qwen's chat markup) encoded as its 24 growing prompts in order on one scratch
(the affinity case): from the second prompt on, tok v1's memo answers every earlier
turn in the pass state too (toksm's likewise), where the markup is added tokens (qwen38, nemotron3-omni: memo
0.90); on glm53 it is
plain text (one segment per prompt), so there it is not a replay. **zh** = wiki-zh + the Gutenberg Chinese novel
(part of cjk). nemotron3-omni is the public stand-in for the incumbent's Nemotron tokenizer.
